<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When creating pull requests, create only ONE per session/issue unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When working with an existing PR, update it with new commits rather than creating additional PRs for the same issue.
* When updating a PR, preserve the original PR title and purpose, updating description only when necessary.
</PULL_REQUESTS>

<PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>
1. EXPLORATION: Thoroughly explore relevant files and understand the context before proposing solutions
2. ANALYSIS: Consider multiple approaches and select the most promising one
3. TESTING:
   * For bug fixes: Create tests to verify issues before implementing fixes
   * For new features: Consider test-driven development when appropriate
   * Do NOT write tests for documentation changes, README updates, configuration files, or other non-functionality changes

<SECURITY>

<ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>
* When user asks you to run an application, don't stop if the application is not installed. Instead, please install the application and run the command again.
* If you encounter missing dependencies:
  1. First, look around in the repository for existing dependency files (requirements.txt, pyproject.toml, package.json, Gemfile, etc.)
  2. If dependency files exist, use them to install all dependencies at once (e.g., `pip install -r requirements.txt`, `npm install`, etc.)
  3. Only install individual packages directly if no dependency files are found or if only specific packages are needed
* Similarly, if you encounter missing dependencies for essential tools requested by the user, install them when possible.
</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

<TROUBLESHOOTING>

  2. Assess the likelihood of each possible cause
  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability

  - Include explanations in your conversation responses rather than creating separate documentation files
  - If you need to create documentation files for reference, do NOT include them in version control unless explicitly requested
  - Never create multiple versions of documentation files with different suffixes
* If the user asks for documentation:
  - Confirm whether they want it as a separate file or just in the conversation
  - Ask if they want documentation files to be included in version control
</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

    25	🐉 Installation
    26	==================
    27	
    28	Install from `PyPI`_:
    29	
    30	.. code:: console
    31	
    32	    $ pipenv install requirementslib
    33	
    34	Install from `Github`_:
    35	
    36	.. code:: console
    37	
    38	    $ pipenv install -e git+https://github.com/sarugaku/requirementslib.git#egg=requirementslib
    39	
    40	
    41	.. _PyPI: https://www.pypi.org/project/requirementslib
    42	.. _Github: https://github.com/sarugaku/requirementslib
    43	
    44	
    45	.. _`Summary`:
    46	
    47	🐉 Summary
    48	============
    49	
    50	RequirementsLib provides a simple layer for building and interacting with
    51	requirements in both the `Pipfile <https://github.com/pypa/pipfile/>`_ format
    52	and the `requirements.txt <https://github.com/pypa/pip/>`_ format.  This library
    53	was originally built for converting between these formats in `Pipenv <https://github.com/pypa/pipenv>`_.
    54	
    55	.. _`Usage`:
    56	
    57	🐉 Usage
    58	=========
    59	
    60	Importing a lockfile into your *setup.py* file
    61	-----------------------------------------------------
    62	
    63	You can use RequirementsLib to import your lockfile into your setup file for including your
    64	**install_requires** dependencies:
    65	
    66	.. code-block:: pycon
    67	
    68	    from requirementslib import Lockfile
    69	    lockfile = Lockfile.create('/path/to/project/dir')
    70	    install_requires = lockfile.as_requirements(dev=False)
    71	
    72	
    73	Interacting with a *Pipfile* directly
    74	-------------------------------------------
    75	
    76	You can also interact directly with a Pipfile:
    77	
    78	.. code-block:: pycon
    79	
    80	    >>> from requirementslib import Pipfile
    81	    >>> pf = Pipfile.load('/home/hawk/git/pypa-pipenv')
    82	    >>> pf.sections
    83	    [Section(name='packages', requirements=[]), Section(name='dev-packages', requirements=[Requirement(name='pipenv', vcs=None, req=FileRequirement(setup_path=None, path='.', editable=True, uri='file:///home/hawk/git/pypa-pipenv', link=<Link file:///home/hawk/git/pypa-pipenv>, name='pipenv', req=<Requirement: "-e file:///home/hawk/git/pypa-pipenv">), markers='', specifiers=None, index=None, editable=True, hashes=[], extras=None),...]
    84	
    85	
    86	And you can even write it back out into Pipfile's native format:
    87	
    88	.. code-block:: pycon
    89	
    90	    >>> print(pf.dump(to_dict=False))
    91	    [packages]
    92	
    93	    [dev-packages]
    94	    pipenv = {path = ".", editable = true}
    95	    flake8 = ">=3.3.0,<4"
    96	    pytest = "*"
    97	    mock = "*"
    98	
    99	    [scripts]
   100	    tests = "bash ./run-tests.sh"
   101	
   102	    [pipenv]
   103	    allow_prereleases = true
   104	
   105	
   106	Create a requirement object from *requirements.txt* format
   107	------------------------------------------------------------------
   108	
   109	.. code-block:: pycon
   110	
   111	    >>> from requirementslib import Requirement
   112	    >>> r = Requirement.from_line('-e git+https://github.com/pypa/pipenv.git@master#egg=pipenv')
   113	    >>> print(r)
   114	    Requirement(name='pipenv', vcs='git', req=VCSRequirement(editable=True, uri='git+https://github.com/pypa/pipenv.git', path=None, vcs='git', ref='master', subdirectory=None, name='pipenv', link=<Link git+https://github.com/pypa/pipenv.git@master#egg=pipenv>, req=<Requirement: "-e git+https://github.com/pypa/pipenv.git@master#egg=pipenv">), markers=None, specifiers=None, index=None, editable=True, hashes=[], extras=[])
   115	
   116	    >>> r.as_pipfile()
   117	    {'pipenv': {'editable': True, 'ref': 'master', 'git': 'https://github.com/pypa/pipenv.git'}}
   118	
   119	
   120	Or move from *Pipfile* format to *requirements.txt*:
   121	
   122	.. code-block:: pycon
   123	
   124	    >>> r = Requirement.from_pipfile(name='pythonfinder', indexes=[], pipfile={'path': '../pythonfinder', 'editable': True})
   125	    >>> r.as_line()
   126	    '-e ../pythonfinder'
   127	
   128	
   129	Resolving Editable Package Dependencies
   130	---------------------------------------------
   131	
   132	Requirementslib also can resolve the dependencies of editable packages by calling the ``run_requires`` method.
   133	This method returns a detailed dictionary containing metadata parsed from the package built in
   134	a transient folder (unless it is already on the system or the call is run in a virtualenv).
   135	
   136	The output of ``run_requires`` is very detailed and in most cases will be sufficient:
   137	
   138	.. code-block:: pycon
   139	
   140	    >>> from pprint import pprint
   141	    >>> from requirementslib.models.requirements import Requirement
   142	    >>> r = Requirement.from_line("-e git+git@github.com:sarugaku/vistir.git#egg=vistir[spinner]")
   143	    >>> setup_info_dict = r.run_requires()
   144	    >>> from pprint import pprint
   145	    >>> pprint(setup_info_dict)
   146	    {'base_dir': '/tmp/requirementslib-t_ftl6no-src/src/vistir',
   147	    'build_backend': 'setuptools.build_meta',
   148	    'build_requires': ['setuptools>=36.2.2', 'wheel>=0.28.0'],
   149	    'extra_kwargs': {'build_dir': '/tmp/requirementslib-t_ftl6no-src/src',
   150	                    'download_dir': '/home/hawk/.cache/pipenv/pkgs',
   151	                    'src_dir': '/tmp/requirementslib-t_ftl6no-src/src',
   152	                    'wheel_download_dir': '/home/hawk/.cache/pipenv/wheels'},
   153	    'extras': {'spinner': [Requirement.parse('cursor'),
   154	                            Requirement.parse('yaspin')],
   155	                'tests': [Requirement.parse('pytest'),
   156	                        Requirement.parse('pytest-xdist'),
   157	                        Requirement.parse('pytest-cov'),
   158	                        Requirement.parse('pytest-timeout'),
   159	                        Requirement.parse('hypothesis-fspaths'),
   160	                        Requirement.parse('hypothesis')]},
   161	    'ireq': <InstallRequirement object: vistir[spinner] from git+ssh://git@github.com/sarugaku/vistir.git#egg=vistir editable=True>,
   162	    'name': 'vistir',
   163	    'pyproject': PosixPath('/tmp/requirementslib-t_ftl6no-src/src/vistir/pyproject.toml'),
   164	    'python_requires': '>=2.6,!=3.0,!=3.1,!=3.2,!=3.3',
   165	    'requires': {'backports.functools_lru_cache;python_version<="3.4"': Requirement.parse('backports.functools_lru_cache; python_version <= "3.4"'),
   166	                'backports.shutil_get_terminal_size;python_version<"3.3"': Requirement.parse('backports.shutil_get_terminal_size; python_version < "3.3"'),
   167	                'backports.weakref;python_version<"3.3"': Requirement.parse('backports.weakref; python_version < "3.3"'),
   168	                'colorama': Requirement.parse('colorama'),
   169	                'pathlib2;python_version<"3.5"': Requirement.parse('pathlib2; python_version < "3.5"'),
   170	                'requests': Requirement.parse('requests'),
   171	                'six': Requirement.parse('six'),
   172	                'spinner': [Requirement.parse('cursor'),
   173	                            Requirement.parse('yaspin')]},
   174	    'setup_cfg': PosixPath('/tmp/requirementslib-t_ftl6no-src/src/vistir/setup.cfg'),
   175	    'setup_py': PosixPath('/tmp/requirementslib-t_ftl6no-src/src/vistir/setup.py')}
   176	
   177	
   178	As a side-effect of calls to ``run_requires``, new metadata is made available on the
   179	requirement itself via the property ``requirement.req.dependencies``:
   180	
   181	
   182	.. code-block:: pycon
   183	
   184	    >>> pprint(r.req.dependencies)
   185	    ({'backports.functools_lru_cache;python_version<="3.4"': Requirement.parse('backports.functools_lru_cache; python_version <= "3.4"'),
   186	    'backports.shutil_get_terminal_size;python_version<"3.3"': Requirement.parse('backports.shutil_get_terminal_size; python_version < "3.3"'),
   187	    'backports.weakref;python_version<"3.3"': Requirement.parse('backports.weakref; python_version < "3.3"'),
   188	    'colorama': Requirement.parse('colorama'),
   189	    'pathlib2;python_version<"3.5"': Requirement.parse('pathlib2; python_version < "3.5"'),
   190	    'requests': Requirement.parse('requests'),
   191	    'six': Requirement.parse('six'),
   192	    'spinner': [Requirement.parse('cursor'), Requirement.parse('yaspin')]},
   193	    [],
   194	    ['setuptools>=36.2.2', 'wheel>=0.28.0'])

   197	🐉 Integrations
   198	==================

   203	
   204	🐉 Contributing
   205	===============
   206	
   207	1. Fork the repository and clone the fork to your local machine: ``git clone git@github.com:yourusername/requirementslib.git``
   208	
   209	2. Move into the repository directory and update the submodules: ``git submodule update --init --recursive``
   210	
   211	3. Install the package locally in a virtualenv using `pipenv <https://github.com/pypa/pipenv>`_: ``pipenv install --dev``
   212	
   213	   a. You can also install the package into a `virtualenv <https://github.com/pypa/virtualenv>`_ by running ``pip install -e .[dev,tests,typing]``
   214	      to ensure all the development and test dependencies are installed
   215	
   216	4. Before making any changes to the code, make sure to file an issue. The best way to ensure a smooth collaboration is to communicate *before*
   217	   investing significant time and energy into any changes! Make sure to consider not just your own use case but others who might be using the library
   218	
   219	5. Create a new branch. For bugs, you can simply branch to ``bugfix/<issuenumber>``. Features can be branched to ``feature/<issuenumber>``. This convention
   220	   is to streamline the branching process and to encourage good practices around filing issues and associating pull requests with specific issues. If you
   221	   find yourself addressing many issues in one pull request, that should give you pause
   222	
   223	6. Make your desired changes. Don't forget to add additional tests to account for your new code -- continuous integration **will** fail without it
   224	
   225	7. Test your changes by running ``pipenv run pytest -ra tests`` or simply ``pytest -ra tests`` if you are inside an activated virtual environment
   226	
   227	8. Create a corresponding ``.rst`` file in the ``news`` directory with a one sentence description of your change, e.g. ``Resolved an issue which sometimes prevented requirements from being converted from Pipfile entries to pip lines correctly``
   228	
   229	9. Commit your changes. The first line of your commit should be a summary of your changes, no longer than 72 characters, followed by a blank line, followed by a bulleted description of your changes.
   230	   Don't forget to add seperate lines with the phrase ``- Fixes #<issuenumber>`` for each issue you are addressing in your pull request
   231	
   232	10. Before submitting your pull request, make sure to ``git remote add upstream git@github.com:sarugaku/requirementslib.git`` and then ``git fetch upstream && git pull upstream master`` to ensure your code is in sync with the latest version of the master branch,
   233	
   234	11. Create a pull request describing your fix, referencing the issues in question. If your commit message from step 8 was detailed, you should be able to copy and paste it
   235	
   236

1.1 Problem Analysis:
- The issue mentions "SetupInfo" class/method which is responsible for building local source packages
- When building a local source package, a temporary build directory is created
- This build directory gets deleted by pip before it can be referenced and inspected for dependencies
- This affects the ability to properly analyze dependencies of local source packages

1.2 Technical Details:
- Class/Method: SetupInfo (likely in setup_info.py)
- Issue: Build directory deletion timing
- Context: Local source directories
- Impact: Dependency inspection fails because build directory is gone

1.3 Clear Problem Statement:
When requirementslib uses SetupInfo to analyze local source packages, it creates a temporary build directory to extract package metadata. However, pip deletes this temporary directory before requirementslib can inspect it for dependencies, leading to incomplete or failed dependency resolution.

1.4 Steps to Reproduce:
1. Have a local source package (directory with setup.py)
2. Use SetupInfo to analyze it 
3. The build process creates a temporary directory
4. Pip deletes the directory before dependency inspection can occur
5. Dependency analysis fails or returns incomplete results

1.5 Best Practices:
- Need to either prevent premature deletion or preserve the build artifacts
- Should maintain compatibility with pip's behavior
- Minimal changes to avoid breaking existing functionality
- Proper cleanup to avoid leaving temporary files</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 2. RUNNING: Install and Run Tests

    14	def read(*parts):
    15	    # intentionally *not* adding an encoding option to open, See:
    16	    #   https://github.com/pypa/virtualenv/issues/201#issuecomment-3145690
    17	    with codecs.open(os.path.join(here, *parts), "r") as fp:
    18	        return fp.read()

    21	def find_version(*file_paths):
    22	    version_file = read(*file_paths)
    23	    version_match = re.search(r"^__version__ = ['\"]([^'\"]*)['\"]", version_file, re.M)
    24	    if version_match:
    25	        return version_match.group(1)
    26	    raise RuntimeError("Unable to find version string.")

  1432	        # type: (TRequirement, Optional[PackageFinder]) -> Optional[SetupInfo]
  1433	        ireq = requirement.as_ireq()
  1434	        subdir = getattr(requirement.req, "subdirectory", None)

  1436	
  1437	    @classmethod
  1438	    @lru_cache()
  1439	    def from_ireq(cls, ireq, subdir=None, finder=None, session=None):
  1440	        # type: (InstallRequirement, Optional[AnyStr], Optional[PackageFinder], Optional[requests.Session]) -> Optional[SetupInfo]
  1441	        import pip_shims.shims
  1442	
  1443	        if not ireq.link:
  1444	            return None
  1445	        if ireq.link.is_wheel:
  1446	            return None
  1447	        if not finder:
  1448	            from .dependencies import get_finder
  1449	
  1450	            session, finder = get_finder()
  1451	        _, uri = split_vcs_method_from_uri(unquote(ireq.link.url_without_fragment))
  1452	        parsed = urlparse(uri)
  1453	        if "file" in parsed.scheme:
  1454	            url_path = parsed.path
  1455	            if "@" in url_path:
  1456	                url_path, _, _ = url_path.rpartition("@")
  1457	            parsed = parsed._replace(path=url_path)
  1458	            uri = urlunparse(parsed)
  1459	        path = None
  1460	        if ireq.link.scheme == "file" or uri.startswith("file://"):
  1461	            if "file:/" in uri and "file:///" not in uri:
  1462	                uri = uri.replace("file:/", "file:///")
  1463	            path = pip_shims.shims.url_to_path(uri)
  1464	        kwargs = _prepare_wheel_building_kwargs(ireq)
  1465	        ireq.source_dir = kwargs["src_dir"]
  1466	        try:
  1467	            is_vcs = ireq.link.is_vcs
  1468	        except AttributeError:
  1469	            try:
  1470	                is_vcs = not ireq.link.is_artifact
  1471	            except AttributeError:
  1472	                is_vcs = False
  1473	        if not (ireq.editable and pip_shims.shims.is_file_url(ireq.link) and is_vcs):
  1474	            if ireq.is_wheel:
  1475	                only_download = True
  1476	                download_dir = kwargs["wheel_download_dir"]
  1477	            else:
  1478	                only_download = False
  1479	                download_dir = kwargs["download_dir"]
  1480	        elif path is not None and os.path.isdir(path):
  1481	            raise RequirementError(
  1482	                "The file URL points to a directory not installable: {}".format(ireq.link)
  1483	            )
  1484	        ireq.ensure_has_source_dir(kwargs["src_dir"])
  1485	        src_dir = ireq.source_dir
  1486	
  1487	        ireq.populate_link(finder, False, False)
  1488	        pip_shims.shims.shim_unpack(
  1489	            link=ireq.link,
  1490	            location=kwargs["src_dir"],
  1491	            download_dir=download_dir,
  1492	            only_download=only_download,
  1493	            session=session,
  1494	            hashes=ireq.hashes(False),
  1495	            progress_bar="off",
  1496	        )
  1497	        created = cls.create(src_dir, subdirectory=subdir, ireq=ireq, kwargs=kwargs)
  1498	        return created
  1499

   346	def build_pep517(source_dir, build_dir, config_settings=None, dist_type="wheel"):
   347	    if config_settings is None:
   348	        config_settings = {}
   349	    requires, backend = get_pyproject(source_dir)
   350	    hookcaller = HookCaller(source_dir, backend)
   351	    if dist_type == "sdist":
   352	        get_requires_fn = hookcaller.get_requires_for_build_sdist
   353	        build_fn = hookcaller.build_sdist
   354	    else:
   355	        get_requires_fn = hookcaller.get_requires_for_build_wheel
   356	        build_fn = hookcaller.build_wheel
   357	
   358	    with BuildEnv() as env:
   359	        env.pip_install(requires)
   360	        reqs = get_requires_fn(config_settings)
   361	        env.pip_install(reqs)
   362	        return build_fn(build_dir, config_settings)
   363	
   364	
   365	@ensure_mkdir_p(mode=0o775)
   366	def _get_src_dir(root):
   367	    # type: (AnyStr) -> AnyStr
   368	    src = os.environ.get("PIP_SRC")
   369	    if src:
   370	        return src
   371	    virtual_env = os.environ.get("VIRTUAL_ENV")
   372	    if virtual_env is not None:
   373	        return os.path.join(virtual_env, "src")
   374	    if root is not None:
   375	        # Intentionally don't match pip's behavior here -- this is a temporary copy
   376	        src_dir = create_tracked_tempdir(prefix="requirementslib-", suffix="-src")
   377	    else:
   378	        src_dir = os.path.join(root, "src")
   379	    return src_dir

   382	@lru_cache()
   383	def ensure_reqs(reqs):
   384	    # type: (List[Union[S, PkgResourcesRequirement]]) -> List[PkgResourcesRequirement]
   385	    import pkg_resources
   386	
   387	    if not isinstance(reqs, Iterable):
   388	        raise TypeError("Expecting an Iterable, got %r" % reqs)
   389	    new_reqs = []
   390	    for req in reqs:
   391	        if not req:
   392	            continue
   393	        if isinstance(req, six.string_types):
   394	            req = pkg_resources.Requirement.parse("{0}".format(str(req)))
   395	        # req = strip_extras_markers_from_requirement(req)
   396	        new_reqs.append(req)
   397	    return new_reqs

"""
Reproduction script for the build locations deletion issue.
"""

import os
import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path

# Add the src directory to the path so we can import requirementslib
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), 'src'))

from requirementslib.models.requirements import Requirement

def test_local_source_package():
    """Test that reproduces the issue with build directory deletion."""
    
    # Copy the environ_config test artifact to a temporary directory
    source_artifact = Path(__file__).parent / "tests" / "artifacts" / "environ_config" / "environ_config"
    
    if not source_artifact.exists():
        print(f"Source artifact not found: {source_artifact}")
        return False
        
    # Create a temporary directory for testing
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        test_artifact = Path(temp_dir) / "environ_config"
        shutil.copytree(str(source_artifact), str(test_artifact))
        
        print(f"Testing with artifact: {test_artifact}")
        print(f"Artifact exists: {test_artifact.exists()}")
        
        # Try to create a requirement from the local source
        try:
            # This should trigger the SetupInfo creation and build process
            req_line = f"-e {test_artifact}"
            print(f"Creating requirement from: {req_line}")
            
            req = Requirement.from_line(req_line)
            print(f"Requirement created: {req}")
            print(f"Requirement name: {req.name}")
            
            # This should trigger the setup_info analysis which builds the package
            if req.req.setup_info:
                print(f"Setup info created: {req.req.setup_info}")
                print(f"Setup info base_dir: {req.req.setup_info.base_dir}")
                
                # Try to get the dependencies
                setup_dict = req.req.setup_info.as_dict()
                print(f"Setup dict keys: {list(setup_dict.keys())}")
                
                if 'requires' in setup_dict:
                    requires = setup_dict['requires']
                    print(f"Dependencies found: {list(requires.keys())}")
                    return True
                else:
                    print("No 'requires' key in setup_dict")
                    return False
            else:
                print("No setup_info found")
                return False
                
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error occurred: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    success = test_local_source_package()

Only needed for ones that are
# globally-managed.
tempdir_kinds = enum(
    BUILD_ENV="build-env",
    EPHEM_WHEEL_CACHE="ephem-wheel-cache",
    REQ_BUILD="req-build",
)


_tempdir_manager = None  # type: Optional[ExitStack]


@contextmanager
def global_tempdir_manager():
    # type: () -> Iterator[None]
    global _tempdir_manager
    with ExitStack() as stack:
        old_tempdir_manager, _tempdir_manager = _tempdir_manager, stack
        try:
            yield
        finally:
            _tempdir_manager = old_tempdir_manager


class TempDirectoryTypeRegistry:
    """Manages temp directory behavior"""

    def __init__(self):
        # type: () -> None
        self._should_delete = {}  # type: Dict[str, bool]

    def set_delete(self, kind, value):
--
        # is the value of the --src option.
        if path is None:
            path = self._create(kind)

        self._path = path
        self._deleted = False
        self.delete = delete
        self.kind = kind

        if globally_managed:
            assert _tempdir_manager is not None
            _tempdir_manager.enter_context(self)

    @property
    def path(self):
        # type: () -> str
        assert not self._deleted, f"Attempted to access deleted path: {self._path}"
        return self._path

    def __repr__(self):
        # type: () -> str
        return f"<{self.__class__.__name__} {self.path!r}>"

    def __init__(
        self,
        path=None,  # type: Optional[str]
        delete=_default,  # type: Union[bool, None, _Default]
        kind="temp",  # type: str
        globally_managed=False,  # type: bool
    ):
        super().__init__()

        if delete is _default:
            if path is not None:
                # If we were given an explicit directory, resolve delete option
                # now.
                delete = False
            else:
                # Otherwise, we wait until cleanup and see what
                # tempdir_registry says.
                delete = None

        # The only time we specify path is in for editables where it
        # is the value of the --src option.
        if path is None:
            path = self._create(kind)

        self._path = path
        self._deleted = False
        self.delete = delete
        self.kind = kind

        if globally_managed:

Let me examine the issue more carefully by looking at how the build process works and when directories get deleted:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the error traceback and the issue description, I now understand the real problem:

1. The issue is that when building local source packages via `SetupInfo`, temporary build directories are created
2. These directories are managed by pip's temp directory system
3. However, pip deletes these temporary directories before requirementslib can reference them for dependency inspection
4. This leads to incomplete or failed dependency resolution

The current test failures are actually masking the real issue because they're failing on pip initialization rather than on the actual build directory deletion problem.

Let me focus on understanding:
1. How build directories are created and managed in SetupInfo
2. When they get deleted by pip
3. How we can prevent premature deletion or preserve the build artifacts

Looking at the code:
- `_prepare_wheel_building_kwargs` creates temporary directories using `create_tracked_tempdir`
- These directories are used in the build process 
- But somehow pip is deleting them before the dependency inspection can happen

The key insight is that we need to either:
1. Prevent pip from deleting these directories prematurely, OR
2. Preserve the build artifacts in a way that survives the directory deletion, OR
3. Change the timing so that dependency inspection happens before deletion

Author: Dan Ryan <dan@danryan.co>
Date:   Sat Nov 23 04:03:39 2019 -0500

    Fix broken functionality related to pip updates

    - Point at new version of `pip-shims`
    - Cut functionality over to rely on shimmed functions where possible
      - This includes in particular the resolver and finder access
    - Update compatibility of VCS access
    - Fixes #191
    - Fix now-broken usage of `Link` objects (fixes #190)
    - Fix test failures due to changes in the `pyparsing` api (fixes #181)
    - Fix `Pipfile` loader by removing now-unneeded validation and relying
      on `plette` (fixes #182, fixes #189)
    - Remove `plette` monkeypatches
    - Fix failed attempts to call `.lower()` on the `validate_ssl` field in
      the `[[source]]` section of a `Pipfile` by casting it as a string
      (fixes #183)
    - Fix failed calls to `Lockfile.as_requirements()` caused by attempting
      to set a property on an `InstallRequirement` that is no longer valid
      (fixes #188)
    - Added support for hiding tokens even without separate user/password
      strings when they appear in a URL provided for a package (fixes #192)

    Signed-off-by: Dan Ryan <dan@danryan.co>

+def update_url_name_and_fragment(name_with_extras, ref, parsed_dict):
+    # type: (Optional[str], Optional[str], Dict[str, Optional[str]]) -> Dict[str, Optional[str]]
+    if name_with_extras:
+        fragment = ""  # type: Optional[str]
+        if parsed_dict["fragment"] is not None:
+            fragment = "{0}".format(parsed_dict["fragment"])
+            if fragment.startswith("egg="):
+                name, extras = pip_shims.shims._strip_extras(name_with_extras)
+                fragment_name, fragment_extras = pip_shims.shims._strip_extras(fragment)
+                if fragment_extras and not extras:
+                    name_with_extras = "{0}{1}".format(name, fragment_extras)
+                fragment = ""
+        elif parsed_dict.get("path") is not None and "&subdirectory" in parsed_dict["path"]:
+            path, fragment = URI.parse_subdirectory(parsed_dict["path"])  # type: ignore
+            parsed_dict["path"] = path
+        elif ref is not None and "&subdirectory" in ref:
+            ref, fragment = URI.parse_subdirectory(ref)
+    if ref:
+        parsed_dict["ref"] = ref.strip()
+    return parsed_dict

+def _get_requires_python(candidate):
+    # type: (Any) -> str
+    requires_python = getattr(candidate, "requires_python", None)
+    if requires_python is not None:
+        link = getattr(candidate, "location", getattr(candidate, "link", None))
+        requires_python = getattr(link, "requires_python", None)
+    return requires_python

+def flatten_pyparsing_exprs(expr):
+    exprs = set()

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Understand the Real Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the issue description and the git history, I think I understand the problem better now:

The issue states: "When building a local source package via `SetupInfo` on master, the build directory created is temporary and is deleted by pip before it can be referenced and inspected for dependencies"

This suggests that:
1. SetupInfo creates temporary build directories to analyze local source packages
2. These directories are somehow deleted by pip before the dependency inspection can complete
3. This leads to incomplete or failed dependency resolution

From looking at the code, I can see that:
- `_prepare_wheel_building_kwargs` creates temporary directories using `create_tracked_tempdir`
- These directories are used in the build process via `build_pep517` function
- The `build_pep517` function uses `BuildEnv()` context manager which might be cleaning up

But actually, looking more carefully at the error and the issue description, I think the problem might be different. Let me re-read the issue:

"Build locations are deleted for local source directories before they can be referenced"

This could mean that when pip unpacks and builds a local source directory, it creates temporary build locations that get deleted before requirementslib can inspect them for dependencies.

Looking at the `from_ireq` method in SetupInfo, I can see:

```python
@classmethod
@lru_cache()
def from_ireq(cls, ireq, subdir=None, finder=None, session=None):
    # ...
    ireq.populate_link(finder, False, False)
    pip_shims.shims.shim_unpack(
        link=ireq.link,
        location=kwargs["src_dir"],
        download_dir=download_dir,
        only_download=only_download,
        session=session,
        hashes=ireq.hashes(False),
        progress_bar="off",
    )
    created = cls.create(src_dir, subdirectory=subdir, ireq=ireq, kwargs=kwargs)
    return created
```

So the flow is:
1. pip unpacks the source to a location (src_dir)
2. SetupInfo.create() is called to analyze the source
3. During analysis, temporary build directories might be created and deleted

The issue might be that the build directories created during the analysis process are temporary and get cleaned up before the dependency inspection can complete.

# Import the necessary modules
from requirementslib.models.setup_info import SetupInfo, _prepare_wheel_building_kwargs
import vistir

def test_build_directory_preservation():
    """Test that demonstrates the build directory deletion issue."""
    
    # Use the environ_config test artifact  
    source_artifact = Path(__file__).parent / "tests" / "artifacts" / "environ_config" / "environ_config"
    
    if not source_artifact.exists():
        print(f"Source artifact not found: {source_artifact}")
        return False
    
    print(f"Source artifact exists: {source_artifact.exists()}")
    
    # Create temporary directories for testing
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_root:
        temp_path = Path(temp_root)
        test_artifact = temp_path / "environ_config"
        shutil.copytree(str(source_artifact), str(test_artifact))
        
        print(f"Copied artifact to: {test_artifact}")
        print(f"Setup.py exists: {test_artifact.joinpath('setup.py').exists()}")
        
        # Prepare wheel building kwargs - this creates temporary directories
        kwargs = _prepare_wheel_building_kwargs()
        print(f"Prepared kwargs with directories:")
        for key, value in kwargs.items():
            print(f"  {key}: {value}")
            if os.path.exists(value):
                print(f"    Directory exists: {os.path.exists(value)}")
            else:
                print(f"    WARNING: Directory does not exist: {value}")
        
        # Create SetupInfo directly
        try:
            setup_info = SetupInfo.create(
                str(test_artifact),
                kwargs=kwargs
            )
            print(f"SetupInfo created successfully")
            print(f"SetupInfo base_dir: {setup_info.base_dir}")
            
            # Check if the build directories still exist
            print("Checking build directories after SetupInfo creation:")
            for key, value in kwargs.items():
                if 'dir' in key and os.path.exists(value):
                    files = list(os.listdir(value)) if os.path.exists(value) else []
                    print(f"  {key} ({value}): exists={os.path.exists(value)}, files={len(files)}")
                elif 'dir' in key:
                    print(f"  {key} ({value}): EXISTS={os.path.exists(value)}")
            
            # Try to get info - this is where the build process happens
            print("Getting setup info (this triggers the build process)...")
            info = setup_info.get_info()
            print(f"Info retrieved successfully")
            print(f"Info keys: {list(info.keys())}")
            
            if 'requires' in info:
                requires = info['requires']
                print(f"Dependencies found: {list(requires.keys()) if requires else 'None'}")
                return len(requires) > 0 if requires else False
            else:
                print("No 'requires' key in info")
                return False
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error during SetupInfo processing: {e}")
            import traceback
            traceback.print_exc()
            return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Testing build directory preservation issue...")
    success = test_build_directory_preservation()
    print(f"\nTest {'PASSED' if success else 'FAILED'}")

    def __init__(
        self,
        path=None,  # type: Optional[str]
        delete=_default,  # type: Union[bool, None, _Default]
        kind="temp",  # type: str
        globally_managed=False,  # type: bool
    ):
        super().__init__()

        if delete is _default:
            if path is not None:
                # If we were given an explicit directory, resolve delete option
                # now.
                delete = False
            else:
                # Otherwise, we wait until cleanup and see what
                # tempdir_registry says.
                delete = None

        # is the value of the --src option.
        if path is None:
            path = self._create(kind)

        self._path = path
        self._deleted = False
        self.delete = delete
        self.kind = kind

    def test_no_duplicate_egg_info():
        """When the package has 'src' directory, do not write egg-info in base dir."""
        base_dir = vistir.compat.Path(os.path.abspath(os.getcwd())).as_posix()
        r = Requirement.from_line("-e {}".format(base_dir))
        egg_info_name = "{}.egg-info".format(r.name.replace("-", "_"))
        distinfo_name = "{0}.dist-info".format(r.name.replace("-", "_"))

        def find_metadata(path):
            metadata_names = [
                os.path.join(path, name) for name in (egg_info_name, distinfo_name)
            ]
            if not os.path.isdir(path):
                return None
            pth = next(iter(pth for pth in metadata_names if os.path.isdir(pth)), None)
            if not pth:
                pth = next(
                    iter(
                        pth
                        for pth in os.listdir(path)
                        if any(
                            pth.endswith(md_ending)
                            for md_ending in [".egg-info", ".dist-info", ".whl"]
                        )
                    ),
                    None,
                )
            return pth

        assert not find_metadata(base_dir)
        assert not find_metadata(os.path.join(base_dir, "reqlib-metadata"))
        assert not find_metadata(os.path.join(base_dir, "src", "reqlib-metadata"))

Let me check what that function does:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/sarugaku__requirementslib__1.5 && python -c "from vistir.path import create_tracked_tempdir; import inspect; print(inspect.getsource(create_tracked_tempdir))"</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>def create_tracked_tempdir(*args, **kwargs):
    # type: (Any, Any) -> str
    """Create a tracked temporary directory.

    This uses `TemporaryDirectory`, but does not remove the directory when
    the return value goes out of scope, instead registers a handler to cleanup
    on program exit.

    The return value is the path to the created directory.
    """

    tempdir = TemporaryDirectory(*args, **kwargs)
    TRACKED_TEMPORARY_DIRECTORIES.append(tempdir)
    atexit.register(tempdir.cleanup)
    warnings.simplefilter("ignore", ResourceWarning)
    return tempdir.name

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me clearly state what I understand about the problem:

5.1 What the problem is:
When requirementslib uses SetupInfo to analyze local source packages, it creates temporary build directories to extract package metadata. However, these temporary directories are being deleted by pip (or some part of the build system) before requirementslib can inspect them for dependencies, leading to incomplete or failed dependency resolution.

5.2 Where the problem is located:
The issue is likely in the interaction between:
- SetupInfo creating temporary build directories via `_prepare_wheel_building_kwargs()` 
- The build process in `build_wheel()` -> `build_pep517()` 
- Some cleanup mechanism that deletes these directories before metadata extraction in `get_metadata_from_wheel()`

5.3 How the test reproduces the issue:
The test tries to create a Requirement from a local source directory, which triggers SetupInfo analysis. During this analysis, temporary build directories are created, but they get deleted before dependency inspection can complete.

5.4 Best practices to take into account:
- Temporary directories should persist long enough for metadata extraction
- Cleanup should happen at the appropriate time (program exit, not immediately)
- The fix should not break existing functionality
- Should be compatible with pip's expectations

5.5 How to fix the problem:
Looking at the code flow:
1. `_prepare_wheel_building_kwargs()` creates temporary directories using `create_tracked_tempdir()` 
2. These should persist until program exit due to the atexit registration
3. But something is deleting them earlier

The fix might be:
- Ensuring the build directories aren't cleaned up prematurely
- Or copying/moving the built artifacts to a persistent location before cleanup
- Or changing the timing of when cleanup happens

But wait, I'm still getting hung up on the pip temp directory manager issue. Let me try to bypass that and focus on the core issue.

Actually, let me re-read the issue description one more time to make sure I understand it correctly.

The issue says: "Build locations are deleted for local source directories before they can be referenced"

This could mean:
1. pip unpacks local source to temporary build locations
2. pip builds them there
3. pip deletes those locations
4. requirementslib tries to reference the built artifacts but they're gone

OR it could mean:
1. requirementslib creates temporary build directories
2. Something (pip?) deletes them before requirementslib can use them

I think it's the first interpretation. Let me look at the `from_ireq` method again.

In `from_ireq`:
1. Creates kwargs with temporary directories
2. Calls `shim_unpack` to put source in src_dir
3. Creates SetupInfo 
4. Later, SetupInfo.build() is called which creates MORE temporary directories for actual building

So there are two sets of temporary directories:
1. src_dir - where the source is unpacked/copied
2. build_dir - where the actual build happens

The issue might be with the build_dir being cleaned up.

But actually, looking at `create_tracked_tempdir`, it uses atexit registration, so cleanup should only happen at program exit.

"""
Debug test to understand the build directory issue.
"""

import os
import sys
import tempfile
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
from contextlib import ExitStack

# Add the src directory to the path
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), 'src'))

# Try to initialize pip's temp directory manager
try:
    from pip._internal.utils.temp_dir import global_tempdir_manager
except ImportError:
    try:
        from pip._internal.utils.temp_dir import TempDirectory
        global_tempdir_manager = TempDirectory._tempdir_manager
    except:
        from contextlib import contextmanager
        @contextmanager
        def global_tempdir_manager():
            yield

from requirementslib.models.setup_info import SetupInfo, _prepare_wheel_building_kwargs
from requirementslib.models.requirements import Requirement

def debug_build_process():
    """Debug the build process to see where directories get deleted."""
    
    # Use the environ_config test artifact  
    source_artifact = Path(__file__).parent / "tests" / "artifacts" / "environ_config" / "environ_config"
    
    if not source_artifact.exists():
        print(f"Source artifact not found: {source_artifact}")
        return False
    
    print(f"Source artifact exists: {source_artifact.exists()}")
    
    # Wrap everything in pip's global tempdir manager
    with global_tempdir_manager():
        # Create temporary directories for testing
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_root:
            temp_path = Path(temp_root)
            test_artifact = temp_path / "environ_config"
            shutil.copytree(str(source_artifact), str(test_artifact))
            
            print(f"Copied artifact to: {test_artifact}")
            print(f"Setup.py exists: {test_artifact.joinpath('setup.py').exists()}")
            
            # Prepare wheel building kwargs - this creates temporary directories
            kwargs = _prepare_wheel_building_kwargs()
            print(f"Prepared kwargs with directories:")
            for key, value in kwargs.items():
                print(f"  {key}: {value}")
                if os.path.exists(value):
                    files = list(os.listdir(value)) if os.path.exists(value) else []
                    print(f"    Directory exists: {os.path.exists(value)}, files={len(files)}")
                else:
                    print(f"    WARNING: Directory does not exist: {value}")
            
            # Check directories before creating SetupInfo
            print("\nDirectories before SetupInfo creation:")
            for key, value in kwargs.items():
                if 'dir' in key:
                    exists = os.path.exists(value)
                    files = len(os.listdir(value)) if exists else 0
                    print(f"  {key}: exists={exists}, files={files}")
            
            return True

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("Debugging build process...")
    success = debug_build_process()
    print(f"\nDebug {'PASSED' if success else 'FAILED'}")

Since I can't easily bypass that issue, let me approach the problem differently. Let me look at the actual issue from the commit history or recent changes.

But first, let me understand what the problem statement is really asking for. The issue says:

"Build locations are deleted for local source directories before they can be referenced"

This suggests that the fix should ensure that build locations persist long enough to be referenced.

Looking at the code, I suspect the issue might be that the temporary build directories created by requirementslib are being cleaned up too early by pip's temp directory management system.

Let me check if there's a way to make the build directories more persistent.

Looking at the `_prepare_wheel_building_kwargs` function, it uses `create_tracked_tempdir` which should register cleanup for program exit, not immediate cleanup.

But maybe the issue is that pip is treating these directories as ephemeral and cleaning them up during the build process.

Let me look at this from a different angle. What if the fix is to ensure that the build artifacts are preserved in a location that won't be cleaned up?

Looking at the code flow:
1. `_prepare_wheel_building_kwargs` creates temporary directories
2. `build_wheel()` or `build_sdist()` builds the package in the build_dir
3. `get_metadata_from_wheel()` tries to read metadata from the built wheel

Maybe the issue is that between steps 2 and 3, something is cleaning up the build_dir.

But actually, looking at `create_tracked_tempdir`, it uses atexit registration, so cleanup should only happen at program exit.

Let me think about what changed recently that might cause this issue. 

Actually, let me just try to implement a potential fix and see if it addresses the issue. 

The problem statement suggests that build locations are deleted before they can be referenced. A common fix for this type of issue would be to:

1. Either prevent the deletion of build directories
2. Or copy/move the built artifacts to a persistent location before deletion can occur
3. Or change the timing of when cleanup happens

Looking at the code, I think the most likely place for the fix is in ensuring that build artifacts are preserved.

Actually, let me re-read the issue description one more time and think about it.

The issue says: "Build locations are deleted for local source directories before they can be referenced"

Maybe the issue is not with requirementslib's temporary directories, but with pip's handling of local source directories.

When pip processes a local source directory, it might:
1. Copy it to a temporary build location
2. Build it there
3. Delete the temporary location
4. But requirementslib tries to reference the build artifacts from the original location

But looking at the `from_ireq` method, it seems like requirementslib is controlling the build process, not pip.

Let me look at this from a different perspective. What if the fix is much simpler?

Looking at the error messages from the failing tests, they're all failing with the pip temp directory manager assertion error, not with build directory deletion.

Maybe the issue description is referring to a problem that manifests as the temp directory manager error.

Let me check if there are any recent issues or pull requests that might give context.

Actually, let me just try to make a reasonable fix based on the problem description.

The issue is: "Build locations are deleted for local source directories before they can be referenced"

A reasonable fix would be to ensure that build locations persist long enough to be referenced.

Looking at the code, build locations are created in `_prepare_wheel_building_kwargs` using `create_tracked_tempdir`. 

But maybe the issue is that these directories are treated as ephemeral by pip's temp directory system.

Let me check if there's a way to make the build process not rely on temporary directories that might get cleaned up.

One approach would be to use persistent directories instead of temporary ones for the build process.

But that might not be the right approach.

Another approach would be to copy the built artifacts to a safe location before they can be deleted.

But let me think about this more systematically.

"</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Function source:
def create_tracked_tempdir(*args, **kwargs):
    # type: (Any, Any) -> str
    """Create a tracked temporary directory.

    This uses `TemporaryDirectory`, but does not remove the directory when
    the return value goes out of scope, instead registers a handler to cleanup
    on program exit.

    The return value is the path to the created directory.
    """

    tempdir = TemporaryDirectory(*args, **kwargs)
    TRACKED_TEMPORARY_DIRECTORIES.append(tempdir)
    atexit.register(tempdir.cleanup)
    warnings.simplefilter("ignore", ResourceWarning)
    return tempdir.name

    if config_settings is None:
        config_settings = {}
    requires, backend = get_pyproject(source_dir)
    hookcaller = HookCaller(source_dir, backend)
    if dist_type == "sdist":
